% whole-plane SLE driver, independently of the one-dimensional Z statistic.
\subsection{Full scaling ergodicity from the stationary cylinder driver}
\label{sec:wp-cylinder-ergodicity}

Zhan's construction in Section 3 of \cite{zhan-tip} gives more than
an ergodic one-dimensional angular statistic once the full cylinder
process is retained.  The following argument supplies the full
discrete-dilation ergodicity premise; it uses the classical
H\"ormander hypoellipticity theorem for the smoothing step.

\begin{proposition}[Every stationary cylinder skeleton is ergodic]
\label{prop:wp-cylinder-ergodic}
Let $\kappa>0$, $\varrho\ge\kappa/2-2$, and
$a=\varrho/2+1>0$.  Let $Z_t\in(0,2\pi)$ be the two-sided stationary
diffusion
\[
 dZ_t=\sqrt\kappa\,dB_t+a\cot(Z_t/2)\,dt,
 \qquad
 \pi_Z(dz)=c\sin(z/2)^{4a/\kappa}\,dz.
 \tag{WP8}
\]
Let $q_0$ be uniform modulo $2\pi$, independent of the entire
stationary path $Z$, and set
\[
 q_t=q_0-\int_0^t\cot(Z_s/2)\,ds\pmod{2\pi}.
 \tag{WP9}
\]
Then the cylinder process $(q_t,Z_t)$ is stationary, and its
two-sided stationary path law is ergodic under every fixed time
shift $h\ne0$.  Consequently any Borel whole-plane Loewner trace
constructed from $e^{i\lambda_t}$, $\lambda_t=q_t+Z_t$, is ergodic
under multiplication by every fixed scalar $e^h\ne1$ as an
unparameterized curve, on a conull invariant set where the trace is defined.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The dimension of the radial Bessel diffusion is
$1+4a/\kappa\ge2$, so its endpoints are not hit; this is the
stationary construction in \cite[Section 3 and Appendix B]{zhan-tip}.
Uniform independent $q_0$ makes $q_t$ uniform conditional on the
entire path $Z$, proving stationarity and giving the invariant
probability $\pi=(dq/(2\pi))\pi_Z(dz)$, with full support.

The Markov generator on the open cylinder is
\[
 \mathcal L=\frac\kappa2\partial_z^2
       +a\cot(z/2)\partial_z-\cot(z/2)\partial_q.
 \tag{WP10}
\]
Write $V_1=\sqrt\kappa\,\partial_z$ and
$V_0=a\cot(z/2)\partial_z-\cot(z/2)\partial_q$.  Then
\[
 [V_1,V_0]
   =\frac{\sqrt\kappa}{2}\csc^2(z/2)
                         (-a\partial_z+\partial_q).
 \tag{WP11}
\]
Thus $V_1$ and $[V_1,V_0]$ span the spatial tangent space at every
interior point.  The parabolic H\"ormander theorem applied to
$\mathcal L-\partial_t$ gives the strong Feller property at every
positive time.  Here is the localization needed for the singular
endpoints.  On a relatively compact subcylinder, the killed backward
semigroup applied to a bounded Borel function solves the homogeneous
backward Kolmogorov equation in the interior at positive times, in
the distributional sense.  H\"ormander's theorem~\cite[Theorem 1.1]{hormander} makes this solution
smooth there.  For any initial interior point and finite horizon,
endpoint nonattainment gives a compact subcylinder containing the
entire path with probability at least $1-\varepsilon$.  Choose a
slightly larger subcylinder.  Coupling nearby starting points with
the same Brownian motion, local Lipschitz continuity and Gronwall's
inequality show that their paths remain in the larger subcylinder
on this same event, for all sufficiently close starting points.
The full semigroup thus differs from that killed semigroup by at
most $\|f\|_\infty\varepsilon$, uniformly near the given point.
Let $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ to obtain continuity for every bounded
Borel $f$.  H\"ormander smoothing is an external analytic input,
not a consequence of stationarity alone.

For clarity, topological irreducibility at every fixed time $h>0$
can be checked directly, without a general support theorem.  Given
two cylinder points $(q,z)$ and $(q',z')$, choose a smooth controlled
path $z(s)$, $0\le s\le h$, from $z$ to $z'$, remaining in a
compact subinterval of $(0,2\pi)$.  It can be chosen so that
\[
 -\int_0^h\cot(z(s)/2)\,ds=q'-q\pmod{2\pi}.
\]
Indeed let the path move to a variable interior level, remain there
for a positive time, and return to $z'$.  As that level ranges from
$0$ to $2\pi$, the integral varies continuously from $-\infty$
to $+\infty$.  The required deterministic Brownian control is
\[
 w(s)=\kappa^{-1/2}\left(z(s)-z-
                     a\int_0^s\cot(z(u)/2)\,du\right).
\]
It is a Cameron--Martin path.  Brownian motion has positive
probability of lying in every uniform neighborhood of $w$.
Local Lipschitz dependence of the additive-noise SDE and its
integrated $q$ coordinate shows that the uncontrolled process ends
in any prescribed neighborhood of $(q',z')$ with positive
probability.  Hence each skeleton kernel $P_h$ is topologically
irreducible.

A strong Feller irreducible Markov kernel has at most one invariant
probability, and its stationary probability is an extreme invariant
probability.  Equivalently its two-sided stationary Markov path
law is shift-ergodic.  Apply this standard Markov-kernel fact to
$P_h$ for every fixed $h>0$.  The same conclusion holds on the
full continuous path space under time shift $h$.  Indeed consider
the Markov chain of path segments $(X_{nh+s})_{0\le s\le h}$,
where $X=(q,Z)$.  Its transition law depends only on the previous
endpoint.  The endpoint law of any invariant segment law must be
invariant for $P_h$, hence must equal $\pi$.  The segment law is
then necessarily the law obtained by starting the diffusion from
$\pi$ and recording its next length-$h$ segment.  Thus the segment
chain has a unique invariant law and is ergodic; concatenation
intertwines its shift with the full continuous-path shift by $h$.
Negative shifts have the same invariant events.

Finally the deterministic whole-plane Loewner correspondence
intertwines shifting $\lambda_t$ to $\lambda_{t+h}$ with mapping
the capacity-parameterized trace $\gamma(t)$ to
$e^{-h}\gamma(t+h)$.  After forgetting the parameter this is
scalar multiplication by $e^{-h}$.  A Borel factor of an ergodic
transformation is ergodic, proving the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Application and remaining premise.}
For $0<\kappa<4$ and $\varrho=\kappa+2$, the stationary
whole-plane SLE$(\kappa;\kappa+2)$ driver of
\cite[Section 3]{zhan-tip} therefore has full dilation ergodicity,
not only the ergodicity of its coordinate $Z$ used in Section 6
of that paper.  Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} still
requires its stated Borel spherical curve coding and proper
continuous trace from $0$ to $\infty$; this proposition supplies
the ergodicity input but does not prove those geometric inputs.

\begin{corollary}[An affine tensor at a deterministic simple-SLE tip]
\label{cor:wp-deterministic-tip-tensor}
Fix $0<\kappa<4$ and assume the proper spherical curve coding
premises of Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} for the whole-plane
SLE$(\kappa;\kappa+2)$ law.  Let $\beta$ be a chordal SLE$_\kappa$
trace, let $t_0>0$ be a deterministic half-plane capacity time,
and let $A\in GL^+(2,\RR)$ be fixed.  The same decoder applied
to the reversed ordered germ of $A\beta$ at $A\beta(t_0)$,
translated to start at $0$, almost surely returns
\[
 [(A^{-1})^{\mathsf T}A^{-1}].
 \tag{WP12}
\]
For a general simply connected domain, use a conformal parameter
chart and its corresponding deterministic chordal capacity time.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Theorem 5.3 of \cite{zhan-tip} maps the reversed past of $\beta$
at $t_0$ by a random conformal map to an initial segment of a
whole-plane SLE$(\kappa;\kappa+2)$ trace.  Its local inverse at
$0$ has a random nonzero conformal derivative $aR$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:wp-cylinder-ergodic} supplies the whole-plane
discrete-dilation ergodicity.  Apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} to this random inverse chart
followed by $A$.  The derivative is $A(aR)$, whose tensor class
is exactly (WP12).  The decoder needs only the initial ordered
germ, so the whole-plane segment can be completed arbitrarily.
\end{proof}

This corollary holds simultaneously at any prescribed countable
set of deterministic capacity times.  It does not assert correctness
at every tip, at a tip chosen by an arbitrary intrinsic rule, or
at a stopping time selected by the trace.



\subsection{An intrinsic measure class without affine Minkowski convergence}
\label{sec:intrinsic-tube-measure}

There is a way to address the measure-selection part of the frontier
problem without first proving existence of Minkowski content after every
affine deformation. This does not supply a typical-tip theorem or a
Borel choice of the relevant local frontier arc.

Fix $0<d<2$. For a nonempty compact planar set $E$ and $\epsilon>0$ put
\[
 m_{E,\epsilon}(dx)=\epsilon^{d-2}
       \mathbf1_{\{\operatorname{dist}(x,E)<\epsilon\}}\,dx.
 \tag{IM1}
\]
Call $E$ content regular if these finite measures converge weakly, as
$\epsilon\downarrow0$, to a finite nonzero measure $\mu_E$.
This is an explicit local Minkowski-content hypothesis. The theorem below
is deterministic; no SLE or exploration assumption enters its proof.

\begin{theorem}[A Euclidean canonical affine-stable measure class]
\label{thm:intrinsic-tube-measure}
There is a Borel map $E\mapsto\nu_E$ from compact planar sets to finite
positive measures, supported on $E$, with the following properties.
It has exact $d$-dimensional similarity covariance: for
$F(x)=sRx+t$, $s>0$ and $R$ orthogonal,
$\nu_{FE}=s^d F_*\nu_E$.
For every content-regular $E$, $\nu_E=\mu_E$. If $A$ is invertible
real-linear, with smallest and largest singular values $a,b>0$, then
\[
 |\det A|\,b^{d-2} A_*\mu_E
       \ \le\ \nu_{AE}\ \le\
 |\det A|\,a^{d-2} A_*\mu_E
 \tag{IM2}
\]
as measures. In particular, $\nu_{AE}$ and $A_*\mu_E$ have precisely
the same null sets. No convergence of the tube measures of $AE$ is
assumed. Exact affine covariance of the selected measure is not claimed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Consider the Borel set of compact sets
\[
 \mathcal E_d=\left\{E:
 0<\liminf_{\epsilon\downarrow0}m_{E,\epsilon}(\mathbb R^2)
   \le\limsup_{\epsilon\downarrow0}m_{E,\epsilon}(\mathbb R^2)<\infty\right\}.
\]
The tube-measure map in (IM1) is Borel in the Hausdorff hyperspace of
compact sets: the distance is jointly continuous in $(E,x)$, so integrals
against bounded continuous test functions are Borel by measurable
integration. The tube measure also depends continuously on positive
$\epsilon$. Indeed every positive distance level has zero area:
if $\operatorname{dist}(x,E)=r>0$, a nearest point of $E$ gives, in
each sufficiently small ball about $x$, a ball of one quarter its radius
where the distance is strictly below $r$. Thus the level set has no
Lebesgue density points. Dominated convergence now proves the asserted
continuity. All small-radius limit operations may consequently be coded
using rational radii, proving that $\mathcal E_d$ is Borel.
For $E\in\mathcal E_d$, the sufficiently small-radius tube measures have
uniformly bounded masses and a common compact support, namely the closed
unit neighborhood of $E$. Their weak cluster set $K(E)$ as $\epsilon\downarrow0$ is therefore
nonempty compact. Every cluster measure is supported on $E$, and its
mass lies between the displayed positive liminf and finite limsup.
Let $C(E)$ be the closed convex hull of $K(E)$; it is compact, since it
lies in the compact set of measures supported on $E$ with the same
uniform mass bounds.

For clarity, these compact-valued constructions admit Borel coding.
Choose a complete metric for weak convergence of finite positive measures.
For every open set $O$ in that measure space,
\[
 K(E)\cap O\ne\varnothing
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{for some }k,\text{ for every }m,\text{ some }
           \epsilon\in\mathbb Q\cap(0,1/m)\\
 \text{has }\operatorname{dist}(m_{E,\epsilon},O^c)>1/k.
 \end{gathered}
 \tag{IM3}
\]
Precompactness proves both implications. The right side is Borel.
Using a countable basis of balls and (IM3), choose a point of $K(E)$
in each basis open set which meets it: successively take the first
smaller basis ball meeting $K(E)$ whose closure lies in the preceding
open ball, with radii tending to zero. The centers form a Cauchy
sequence; its limit lies in $K(E)$ and in the original basis open set.
All choices are least-integer choices of Borel predicates.
This gives a Borel countable dense family in $K(E)$, and its rational
convex combinations give a Borel countable dense family in $C(E)$.

Select $\nu_E$ to minimize the Gaussian energy
\[
 J_E(\nu)=\iint e^{-|x-y|^2/\operatorname{diam}(E)^2}\,\nu(dx)\nu(dy)
 \tag{IM4}
\]
over $C(E)$. The diameter is positive on $\mathcal E_d$, since a singleton
has tube mass of order $\epsilon^d\to0$. The energy is continuous for weak convergence of finite
positive measures, and its dependence on $E$ is Borel. It is strictly convex: for a nonzero finite signed
measure $\eta$, the Gaussian Fourier identity gives
\[
 \iint e^{-|x-y|^2/\operatorname{diam}(E)^2}\,\eta(dx)\eta(dy)
   =c_E\int e^{-\operatorname{diam}(E)^2|\xi|^2/4}
                  |\widehat\eta(\xi)|^2d\xi>0,
\]
where $c_E>0$. The last inequality
uses continuity and uniqueness of the Fourier transform of a finite
measure. Thus the minimizer exists and is unique. It is Borel in $E$:
the infimum over the countable dense family in $C(E)$ is Borel;
choose its first member within $1/j$ of that infimum. Compactness and
uniqueness force these approximate minimizers to converge to $\nu_E$.
Set $\nu_E=0$ outside $\mathcal E_d$, also for the empty set.

For a similarity $F(x)=sRx+t$, the tube change of variables gives
$m_{FE,\epsilon}=s^d F_*m_{E,\epsilon/s}$. The full-radius cluster
construction therefore gives $C(FE)=s^d F_*C(E)$. Also
$J_{FE}(s^dF_*\nu)=s^{2d}J_E(\nu)$. Uniqueness gives the stated
exact similarity covariance.
If $E$ is content regular, $K(E)=C(E)=\{\mu_E\}$.

For the affine comparison, the singular-value bounds give tube inclusions
\[
 A(E_{\epsilon/b})\subset(AE)_\epsilon
                         \subset A(E_{\epsilon/a}),
\]
where $E_r$ is the open radius-$r$ neighborhood. Hence for every bounded
nonnegative continuous $f$,
\[
 |\det A|\,b^{d-2}
 m_{E,\epsilon/b}(f\circ A)
 \le m_{AE,\epsilon}(f)
 \le |\det A|\,a^{d-2}
 m_{E,\epsilon/a}(f\circ A).
 \tag{IM5}
\]
Full tube convergence for $E$ shows that every weak cluster
measure of $AE$ satisfies (IM2). The total masses are bounded above
and away from zero, so $AE\in\mathcal E_d$. The measure inequalities
are preserved by convex combinations and weak limits. They therefore
hold for every element of $C(AE)$, in particular its selected minimizer.
Testing against nonnegative continuous functions proves the inequalities
as Borel measures and finishes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Removing the randomness of an intrinsic-point decoder]
\label{cor:intrinsic-point-decoder}
Suppose a compact trace is an affine image $F=AE$ of a content-regular
trace, and $\Theta(F,x)$ is a Borel local-arc tensor observation, with a
cemetery value permitted. If for one tensor $Q$,
\[
 \Theta(F,x)=Q\quad\text{for }A_*\mu_E\text{-almost every }x,
 \tag{IM6}
\]
then the same equality holds for $\nu_F$-almost every $x$.
Moreover $Q$ is recoverable as a Borel function of $F$ alone whenever
(IM6) holds, without choosing a capacity time or a random point.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The null-set assertion is (IM2). On the Borel set where
$\nu_F(\mathbb R^2)>0$, push $\nu_F/\nu_F(F)$ forward by
$x\mapsto\Theta(F,x)$; outside it return the cemetery value. This is a Borel probability kernel in $F$ and,
under (IM6), equals $\delta_Q$. The Dirac embedding of the Polish tensor
space into its probability-measure space is Borel injective and has a
Borel inverse on its image. Apply that inverse if the pushed measure is
Dirac, and otherwise return the cemetery value. This constructs the
claimed deterministic Borel decoder.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Known natural-length sampling and the gap that remains.}
Lawler--Rezaei~\cite{lawler-rezaei-content} proves existence of SLE
Minkowski content and identifies natural parametrization. Zhan's
decomposition theorem~\cite{zhan-natural-palm}, Theorem 4.1 and
Corollaries 4.2--4.3, identifies the chordal-SLE curve--point measure
obtained from Minkowski-content sampling with the integral of two-sided
radial SLE through $z$ against its Green-function density. Thus a
natural-length Palm decomposition is available, and is different from
the deterministic-capacity-tip theorem in~\cite{zhan-tip}.
To use (IM6) one still needs a Borel extraction of the appropriate
ordered frontier arc and a decoder valid at its natural-length typical
point. A capacity-almost-everywhere statement alone supplies neither.
The robust random-chart decoder reduces the remaining model question
to its explicit properness, isotropy, scale-ergodicity, and local-chart
premises for that intrinsic-point law. No such frontier identification
or all-stopping-time compatibility is asserted here.



\subsection{The tensor at a natural-length typical point of a simple SLE}
\label{sec:natural-point-tensor}

The natural-length Palm law uses a different whole-plane model from the
deterministic-capacity tip law. The stationary cylinder argument applies
also to this model, with force parameter $\varrho=2$.
We restrict to $0<\kappa<4$ so that the relevant traces are simple.
Let $\mu_2$ be the whole-plane SLE$(\kappa;2)$ law from $0$ to
$\infty$. Continuous proper traces for this explicit SLE model are established
in \cite[Theorem 1.12 and Proposition 2.5]{ms-ig4}. Retain the spherical
curve coding and Borel local stopping/completion premises of Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder}.
Proposition~\ref{prop:wp-cylinder-ergodic}, with $\varrho=2$, supplies
its full discrete-dilation ergodicity.

Call a source-avoiding ordered germ at $0$ $\mu_2$-generic if a proper
source-avoiding completion $Y$ has
\[
 \frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N\delta_{\rho^nY}\ \Longrightarrow\ \mu_2
 \quad\text{for one fixed }\rho>1.
 \tag{NP1}
\]
By tail replacement this does not depend on the completion and is a
Borel germ property. It is stronger than merely assigning a tensor
value to an arbitrary decoder. The spherical linearization argument
shows pathwise that this property is preserved by every local
conformal map fixing $0$ with nonzero derivative: the derivative is a
similarity, and $\mu_2$ is rotation and dilation invariant. More
generally applying any fixed affine $A$ makes the limit $A_*\mu_2$.
These assertions hold simultaneously for sample-dependent local
conformal maps, without another exceptional-set intersection.

\begin{proposition}[The terminal germ of radial SLE$(\kappa;2)$]
\label{prop:radial-two-terminal-germ}
For every $\theta\in(0,2\pi)$, take radial SLE$(\kappa;2)$ in
$\mathbb D$ from $e^{i\theta}$ to $0$, with boundary force point $1$.
Its reversed terminal ordered germ at $0$ is $\mu_2$-generic almost
surely. The same assertion holds in any conformally equivalent marked
domain, using the physical Euclidean coordinate at its interior target.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
We use whole-plane SLE$(\kappa;2)$ reversibility, proved in
\cite[Theorem 1.20]{ms-ig4}, and its conformal Markov property;
the relation with radial SLE$(\kappa;2)$ is recalled explicitly in
\cite[Section 2.1]{zhan-natural-regularity} and
\cite[Section 2.2]{zhan-loops}. These are external SLE inputs.

First construct a full stationary whole-plane SLE$(\kappa;2)$ trace
$\gamma$ from $0$ to $\infty$, parametrized by whole-plane capacity,
with Loewner maps $g_t$ and cylinder coordinates $\lambda_t,q_t$.
Let $Z_t=\lambda_t-q_t\in(0,2\pi)$.
Under inversion $J(z)=1/z$, $\Gamma=J\gamma$ is a whole-plane
SLE$(\kappa;2)$ from $\infty$ to $0$. At time $0$, conditional
on its explored past, the remaining path is radial SLE$(\kappa;2)$
in the remaining domain, from its current tip to $0$, with force
point at the prime end corresponding to $\infty$.
The conformal chart
\[
 f(z)=\frac{e^{iq_0}}{g_0(1/z)}
 \tag{NP2}
\]
maps that domain to the unit disk, fixes $0$, takes the force point
to $1$, and takes the tip to $e^{-iZ_0}$. It is analytic with nonzero
derivative at $0$. The normalization at capacity zero gives
$f'(0)=e^{iq_0}$.

Reversibility makes the full reversed $\Gamma$ a whole-plane
SLE$(\kappa;2)$ from $0$ to $\infty$. Its initial germ is therefore
$\mu_2$-generic almost surely. Applying the sample-dependent chart
$f$ preserves (NP1). The resulting reversed terminal germ belongs,
conditionally on the past, to the radial law in the statement with
angle $\theta=-Z_0$ modulo $2\pi$.
The stationary gap density is
\[
 \pi_Z(dz)=c\sin(z/2)^{8/\kappa}\,dz,
 \tag{NP3}
\]
which is positive on $(0,2\pi)$. Disintegration therefore proves the
proposition for Lebesgue-almost every starting angle $\theta$.

To obtain every angle, start the radial law at an arbitrary
$\theta_0\in(0,2\pi)$ and run it for a deterministic radial time
$s>0$. After mapping by its radial Loewner map and rotating to put
the current force point at $1$, its future is a radial law with
gap $X_s$, where
\[
 dX_t=\sqrt\kappa\,dB_t+2\cot(X_t/2)\,dt,
 \qquad X_0=\theta_0.
 \tag{NP4}
\]
The endpoint Bessel dimension is $1+8/\kappa>2$, so neither endpoint
is reached. The distribution of $X_s$ is absolutely continuous with
respect to Lebesgue measure: on each compact subinterval of $(0,2\pi)$,
the stopped drift is bounded, and Girsanov compares its endpoint law
with Brownian motion on the event of no exit. These no-exit events
increase to probability one as the compact intervals exhaust
$(0,2\pi)$. Thus $X_s$ lies in the already proved good-angle set
almost surely. Conditional DMP gives (NP1) for the future terminal
germ in its new chart. The inverse radial chart is analytic near $0$
with nonzero conformal derivative, so pathwise conformal invariance
of (NP1) transfers the property back to the original terminal germ.
For a general marked domain use an interior conformal chart at the
target and the same pathwise invariance. No continuity of the good
event as a function of $\theta$ is assumed.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A natural-length almost-everywhere affine tensor decoder]
\label{thm:natural-point-tensor}
Let $\beta$ be chordal SLE$_\kappa$, $0<\kappa<4$, in a simply
connected domain, with its Minkowski-content measure $\mu_\beta$.
Let $A\in GL^+(2,\mathbb R)$ be fixed. Use the decoder of
Theorem~\ref{thm:wp-robust-decoder} constructed from the model $\mu_2$.
Almost surely, for $\mu_\beta$-almost every interior point
$z=\beta(t)$, the reversed incoming ordered germ of $A\beta$ at $Az$,
translated to start at $0$, has decoded tensor
\[
 [(A^{-1})^{\mathsf T}A^{-1}].
 \tag{NP5}
\]
The same holds for the outgoing germ. This is a statement about the
intrinsic length measure, not capacity-almost every time.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Zhan's natural-length Palm decomposition
\cite[Theorem 4.1 and Corollaries 4.2--4.3]{zhan-natural-palm}
identifies chordal SLE with a point sampled using its Minkowski content
with two-sided radial SLE through that point, integrated against the
SLE Green-function density. For every marked interior point $z$, the
incoming arm of the two-sided radial curve is radial SLE$(\kappa;2)$
from the source to $z$, with force point at the remote boundary target.
Proposition~\ref{prop:radial-two-terminal-germ} makes its reversed
terminal germ $\mu_2$-generic. Hence the affine-image empirical law
is $A_*\mu_2$, which the robust decoder identifies as (NP5).
The bad-point indicator is jointly Borel in $(\beta,z)$: on a
simple representative the point $z$ has a unique hit (up to pauses),
whose first-hit cut is Borel; restriction, reversal, translation, and
the germ decoder with its Borel completion are Borel. One may first
cut a branch inside $B(z,\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial D)/2)$,
so rough source or target boundaries do not enter this observation.
Integrating the Palm identity over compact interior sets, followed by
a countable exhaustion, proves that the expected Minkowski-content
mass of bad points is zero. That mass is nonnegative, so it vanishes
almost surely. Reversibility of two-sided radial SLE, recalled in
\cite[Section 2.2]{zhan-loops}, gives the outgoing assertion.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{What this removes and what remains.}
For compact simple-SLE pieces satisfying the content-regularity premise
of Theorem~\ref{thm:intrinsic-tube-measure}, (NP5) supplies (IM6).
Consequently a tensor can be recovered from their unlabelled trace by
the intrinsic-measure construction, provided the local incoming or
outgoing branch is Borel extracted from that trace. A simple compact
arc has two possible orientations; (NP5) works for both, so choosing
an orientation does not change its almost-everywhere tensor.
This does not yet identify the laws of all accessible frontier arcs of
an exploration, establish the content premise on a chosen frontier
exhaustion, or prove compatibility at every random slit state and
every exploration stopping time. It does not derive an affine-SLE
representation from the three axioms.



\subsection{A tensor from an unlabelled compact simple-SLE arc}
\label{sec:unlabelled-simple-arc}

The natural-point theorem and the intrinsic measure can be combined
without leaving an unproved choice of orientation for a compact simple
arc. We give the coding and local-content details explicitly. This
result concerns arcs already known to be simple SLE; it does not identify
arbitrary accessible boundary pieces of an unknown exploration.

\begin{lemma}[Borel orientation and cutting of simple compact arcs]
\label{lem:borel-simple-arc}
Use the space $\mathcal C_c$ of arbitrary compact planar oriented
curves, with pauses identified and the Fr\'echet metric. This is a
separate space from the fixed-endpoint spherical $0$-to-$\infty$ space.
Use its continuous quotient map from $C([0,1],\mathbb R^2)$.
The nonconstant simple-arc subspace is Borel. Its trace map to the
Hausdorff hyperspace of compact planar sets has exactly two elements
in each nonempty fiber. Consequently its image is Borel and there is
a Borel choice of an orientation for every compact simple arc. Given
an oriented simple arc and a point in its trace, cutting there and
reversing either resulting branch are jointly Borel operations.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The compact planar curve space is Polish: images of rational
polygonal paths give separability. For completeness, align the
representatives of a Cauchy subsequence successively by increasing
homeomorphisms so that their successive uniform distances are
summable. Their uniform limit is continuous and represents the
Fr\'echet limit; the original Cauchy sequence has the same limit.
The quotient map is continuous.

For continuous representatives, failure of simplicity modulo pauses is
the existence of $r\le s\le t$ with
\[
 \gamma(r)=\gamma(t),\qquad
             |\gamma(s)-\gamma(r)|\ge1/n
 \tag{UA1}
\]
for some integer $n\ge1$. For fixed $n$ this is a closed condition:
the three times range over a compact set and subsequential limits
preserve both relations. These conditions are invariant under the
identification of pauses. Their images and their complements under
the continuous Polish quotient are analytic, hence Borel. Excluding
their countable union and the constant curves gives the claimed
Borel subspace. The absence of a nonconstant closed subpath is
equivalent to being an embedded arc after its constant intervals are
collapsed.

The trace map is continuous for Fr\'echet and Hausdorff metrics.
An embedded compact arc has exactly two orientations, so its
restriction to this Borel subspace is two-to-one. The countable-fiber
Borel uniformization theorem gives both a Borel image and a Borel
orientation selector. For cutting, the unique point hit (or its
constant interval in a representative with pauses) is a Borel first
hitting time. Indeed its sublevel events are given by the minimum
distance on a compact initial interval being zero. Restriction and
reversal are Borel on representatives, invariant after quotienting,
and therefore descend by the same analytic/complement argument.
Endpoints or a constant branch may be assigned a cemetery observation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Segment content implies weak local tube convergence]
\label{lem:segment-content-weak}
Let $y:[s,t]\to\mathbb R^2$ be a simple continuous curve, and let
$\Theta$ be continuous and strictly increasing. Suppose for every
$s\le u<v\le t$ the $d$-dimensional total Minkowski content of
$y([u,v])$ exists and equals $\Theta(v)-\Theta(u)$.
Then $E=y([s,t])$ is content regular in the sense of (IM1), with
\[
 \mu_E=y_*\bigl(d\Theta|_{[s,t]}\bigr).
 \tag{UA2}
\]
In particular the simultaneous segment-content theorem of
Lawler--Rezaei~\cite[Theorem 1.1]{lawler-rezaei-content} supplies this
property for every compact nontrivial interior time segment of a
simple chordal SLE in $\mathbb H$, on one probability-one set.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a finite partition $s=t_0<\cdots<t_m=t$, set
$E_j=y([t_{j-1},t_j])$. The tube measures satisfy
$m_{E,\epsilon}\le\sum_jm_{E_j,\epsilon}$. The difference is
nonnegative, and its total mass tends to
\[
 \sum_j\{\Theta(t_j)-\Theta(t_{j-1})\}
                     -\{\Theta(t)-\Theta(s)\}=0.
\]
Thus replacing $m_{E,\epsilon}$ by the sum costs $o(1)$ against
any bounded continuous test $f$. Choose the partition fine enough
that each $E_j$ has diameter at most $\delta$. On its radius-$\epsilon$
tube, $f$ differs from $f(y(t_{j-1}))$ by at most its modulus of
continuity at $\delta+2\epsilon$ on a fixed compact neighborhood
of $E$. The tube masses converge to the corresponding increments
of $\Theta$. Riemann--Stieltjes sums give (UA2) by first taking
$\epsilon\downarrow0$ and then $\delta\downarrow0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Simultaneous affine decoding from every compact simple-SLE subarc]
\label{thm:unlabelled-simple-arc}
Fix $0<\kappa<4$, put $d=1+\kappa/8$, and retain the stated
Polish spherical curve and Borel completion coding.
There is a Borel map $\mathcal T_\kappa$ from compact planar sets
to projective positive definite tensors, with a cemetery value,
such that the following holds. If $\beta$ is chordal SLE$_\kappa$
in $\mathbb H$, then on one probability-one set, simultaneously
for every $A\in GL^+(2,\mathbb R)$ and every $0<s<t<\infty$,
\[
 \mathcal T_\kappa\bigl(A\beta([s,t])\bigr)
        =[(A^{-1})^{\mathsf T}A^{-1}].
 \tag{UA3}
\]
The input is only the unlabelled compact trace. Neither its
orientation, capacity time, source, nor target is supplied.
In particular $A,s,t$ may be selected as functions of the sample
on this conull set.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:natural-point-tensor} gives more
than correctness for a single fixed affine map: on one conull
set, at content-almost every point of $\beta$, both local
ordered branches are $\mu_2$-generic before affine deformation.
For each such germ, (NP1) and commutation with linear maps
give the affine empirical law $A_*\mu_2$ simultaneously for
all $A$. No uncountable intersection of probability-one events
is needed. Intersect with the simultaneous segment-content
event of Lawler--Rezaei.

For any compact simple arc $F$, choose its orientation by
Lemma~\ref{lem:borel-simple-arc}. At each interior point $x$,
apply the $\mu_2$ tensor decoder to its reversed incoming branch
translated to start at $0$, using a Borel radial completion.
Call this observation $\Theta(F,x)$. A branch can be cut inside
a small ball about $x$ before completing it, so only its germ
enters. This is a Borel observation by the lemma and the
previously established Borel germ decoder.

Set $E=\beta([s,t])$ and $F=AE$ on the conull set above.
Lemma~\ref{lem:segment-content-weak} makes $E$ content regular,
with positive finite atomless measure $\mu_E$. By simplicity and
the natural parametrization identity, $\mu_E$ is exactly the
restriction of the full natural measure $\mu_\beta$ to $E$.
At $A_*\mu_E$-almost every point of $F$, the chosen incoming
branch is one of the two already good branches of the full
curve. Its observation is therefore the right side of (UA3),
regardless of the sample-dependent orientation choice.
Theorem~\ref{thm:intrinsic-tube-measure} makes $\nu_F$ equivalent
to $A_*\mu_E$. Apply the Dirac-pushforward construction of
Corollary~\ref{cor:intrinsic-point-decoder} to $\Theta(F,\cdot)$.
Return the cemetery value off the Borel simple-arc set or when
the selected measure vanishes or the pushforward is not a
single non-cemetery Dirac mass. This defines the required
Borel map and proves (UA3).

All comparisons after the two conull events were deterministic,
and their statements hold for every affine map and every
subinterval. This proves the simultaneous assertion.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Limit of this advance.}
This provides an intrinsic measure and orientation-independent decoder
on known affine simple-SLE arcs, including sample-dependent subarcs.
It does not assert that an arbitrary accessible frontier decomposes
into such arcs with Borel extraction, identify those arc laws for
the unknown exploration family, or supply continuation kernels at
every random slit state. No classification follows without those steps.


% Research fragment from nonsimple_restart; no tracked files edited.
\section{Restart states and direct driving bounds for nonsimple traces}
\label{sec:nonsimple-direct-bounds}

\paragraph{Scope.}
The exploration itself is not assumed to contain any embedded time
interval. It is an ordered, possibly densely self-touching curve. A
decoder theorem for simple SLE arcs is a separate statement about
different objects. At a finite capacity time $\tau$, its state is the
remaining target component with its inherited colored boundary and its
distinguished source and target \emph{prime ends}, not just the two
Euclidean endpoint locations. If $f:\mathbb H\to D$ is the initial
inverse chart, its restarted inverse chart is
\[
 f_\tau(z)=f\bigl(g_\tau^{-1}(z+W_\tau)\bigr).
 \tag{NS1}
\]
The centered future driver is $W_{\tau+s}-W_\tau$. This identity uses
Loewner composition and does not assert conformal covariance of the
unknown physical family. The marked-state space is required to be Borel,
and genuine strong DMP is a hypothesis on this state space. The chart
is coordinate bookkeeping, not an extra physical state variable:
the law in the physical domain does not depend on its choice.

Uniform approximation of a deterministic ordered curve by simple
interior paths does not by itself assert strict growth of its filled
hulls. The topological characterization in Yuan~\cite{yuan}, Theorem 1.1 and
Remark 1.4, additionally requires strictly increasing filled hulls.
For the results below continuous Loewner generation and the usual
capacity clock are standing admissibility hypotheses. No stochastic
semimartingale property is included in admissibility. Initially the
capacity lifetime may be $\zeta\le\infty$; strong DMP and all
restart assertions are imposed on $\{\tau<\zeta\}$ for stopping times
$\tau$, allowing $\tau=\infty$ elsewhere. The full curve tends to its target prime end, so the charted
trace tends to infinity as capacity tends to $\zeta$.

\paragraph{A continuum crossing assumption.}
Fix $M>0$ and $0<q<1$. At every admissible marked state, suppose that
every nonseparating boundary quadrilateral of modulus at least $M$
has exploration crossing probability at most $q$. Here the two side
arcs of the quadrilateral lie on the domain boundary, the two crossed
arcs lie in the domain, and nonseparating means that the source and
target can be connected while avoiding the quadrilateral. All crossing
events, admissible pairs of states and quads, and evaluation kernels
are assumed jointly Borel. The constants are uniform over the state
class, including its random slit states. This is a precise upper
unforced-crossing input; it is not a substitute for a two-sided
configuration-level RSW hypothesis. We use a boundary-robust
crossing convention: the joining subcurve may lie in the closed
quadrilateral and may touch its two boundary side arcs between its
crosscut hits. Closures at boundary sides are interpreted with their
inherited prime-end sides. In particular, a nonsimple trace which
joins the two semicircles of a half-annulus while touching the real
line is a crossing. An estimate stated only for subcurves strictly
inside the open quad is not silently substituted for this assumption.

\begin{lemma}[Time-zero crossing bounds become conditional bounds]
\label{lem:ns-timezero-conditional}
Suppose a Borel family satisfies genuine strong DMP on the stated
restart class and the preceding time-zero crossing assumption at every
state. For every stopping time $\tau$, on $\{\tau<\zeta\}$, and every
$\mathcal F_\tau$-measurable admissible nonseparating quad $Q_\tau$
of modulus at least $M$ in the remaining state,
\[
 \mathbb P\{\hbox{future exploration crosses }Q_\tau
                   \mid\mathcal F_\tau\}\le q
       \quad\hbox{almost surely}.
 \tag{NS2}
\]
The same bound holds in the restarted half-plane chart, with the same
constants.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $X_\tau$ be the marked state and let $P_x$ be the exploration
kernel. Strong DMP identifies the conditional crossing probability
with $P_{X_\tau}(\hbox{cross }Q_\tau)$. The numerical time-zero
bound holds for every admissible pair $(x,Q)$, so evaluation at the
random pair gives (NS2). This does not intersect an uncountable
collection of almost-sure statements. Conformal maps preserve the
quad modulus, its separation status, and the crossing event.
Consequently its pullback to the physical state has exactly the same
bound. We have transported a geometric inequality, not identified the
law of a conformal image with a law in another physical domain.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A deterministic driver localization bound]
\label{lem:ns-driver-disk}
Let a continuous chordal Loewner trace have filled hulls $K_s$ and
driver $W_s$, with $\operatorname{hcap}(K_s)=2s$. If
$K_t\subset\{z:|z|\le R\}$, then
\[
 \sup_{0\le s\le t}|W_s|\le3R.
 \tag{NS3}
\]
Also $\sup\{\operatorname{Im}z:z\in K_t\}\le2\sqrt t$.
These facts do not require a simple trace.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For fixed $s\le t$, Schwarz-reflect the hydrodynamic map $g_s$
across the real line outside $[-R,R]$. On $|z|>R$ it is univalent
and has expansion $g_s(z)=z+\sum_{n\ge1}b_nz^{-n}$.
The area theorem applied after rescaling by $R$ gives
\[
 \sum_{n\ge1}\frac{n|b_n|^2}{R^{2n+2}}\le1.
\]
Consequently Cauchy--Schwarz gives, at $z=\pm2R$,
\[
 |g_s(z)-z|\le R\left(\sum_{n\ge1}\frac1{n4^n}\right)^{1/2}<R.
\]
The real interval which is the image of the two sides of the hull,
and in particular its current tip image $W_s$, lies between
$g_s(-2R)$ and $g_s(2R)$ by boundary order. This proves (NS3).
For the height bound, while $z$ is unswallowed its image height $Y_s$
satisfies
\[
 \partial_sY_s=-\frac{2Y_s}{|g_s(z)-W_s|^2},\qquad
 \partial_sY_s^2\ge-4.
\]
A point of original height greater than $2\sqrt t$ therefore cannot
be swallowed by time $t$. The closed-hull bound follows by closure.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional Brownian-size driving bounds without simplicity]
\label{prop:ns-driver-exponential}
For a continuous-driver family satisfying the hypotheses of
Lemma~\ref{lem:ns-timezero-conditional}, whose full traces tend to the
target prime end (and hence to infinity in their half-plane chart),
the capacity lifetime is infinite almost surely. There are $c,C>0$, depending
only on $M,q$, such that, for every finite stopping time $\tau$,
$h>0$, and $x\ge0$,
\[
 \mathbb P\left\{\sup_{0\le s\le h}|W_{\tau+s}-W_\tau|
                         >x\sqrt h\mid\mathcal F_\tau\right\}
      \le Ce^{-cx}.
 \tag{NS4}
\]
In particular, for some $a,C'>0$,
\[
 \mathbb E\left[\left.
   \exp\left\{a\sup_{0\le s\le h}
       |W_{\tau+s}-W_\tau|/\sqrt h\right\}
                    \right|\mathcal F_\tau\right]\le C'.
 \tag{NS5}
\]
For every $p>0$ this gives a constant $C_p$ with conditional
$p$-th moment at most $C_ph^{p/2}$. The driver is almost surely
locally $\alpha$-H\"older for every $\alpha<1/2$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Work first at time zero in the half-plane chart, before the possible
capacity lifetime. Fix $u>0$ and choose $d=4e^{\pi M}$. To bound
horizontal travel before reaching height $u$, use the successive first hitting
times of the lines $\operatorname{Re}z=kdu$, $k=1,2,\ldots$, before
upper exit. At the first hit of line $kdu$, all the earlier trace and
its filled hull lie to its left. The half-annulus centered at
$(k+1)du$, of inner radius $2u$ and outer radius $du/2$, lies entirely
to the right of that line and is untouched. As a boundary quad its
modulus for crossing between the semicircles is
\[
 \pi^{-1}\log\frac{du/2}{2u}=M.
\]
It does not separate the current source prime end from infinity.
Indeed, a source access arc eventually lies near the tip, to the left
of this quad. Any path from such an access point to infinity first
enters the empty right half-plane to the left of the quad, where it
can be rerouted around its outer semicircle. This verifies the
prime-end separation statement without equating prime ends which
have the same physical impression. Reaching the next line at height
at most $u$ requires
crossing this quad from its outer to its inner semicircle. Its
conditional probability is therefore at most $q$ by (NS2).
The same construction starts at $k=0$ with the source at zero.

Iterating gives probability at most $q^n$ for hitting line $ndu$
before upper exit. Reflection of this geometric construction treats
left exit, without assuming reflection invariance of the laws. If
the capacity lifetime were at most a fixed $T<\infty$, the height
bound would confine the full trace to height $2\sqrt T$, while
convergence to infinity would require unbounded horizontal extent.
Use the preceding estimate with $u=3\sqrt T$, so that no upper exit
is possible on this event.
The iterated crossing estimate makes that event have probability zero.
A countable union over integer $T$ proves infinite lifetime.

Now put $u=2\sqrt h$. While the trace stays in
$[-L,L]\times[0,u]$, each filled hull lies in the disk of radius
$\sqrt{L^2+u^2}$. Lemma~\ref{lem:ns-driver-disk} implies that, for
$L\ge u$, a driver supremum greater than $5L$ requires a horizontal
trace exit before time $h$; the height bound excludes upper exit.
The probability of reaching either side $\pm L$ before upper exit
is at most $2q^{\lfloor L/(du)\rfloor}$. Taking
$L=x\sqrt h/5$ when $x\ge10$, and enlarging the prefactor for
$x<10$, proves (NS4) at zero. The proof only used target-visible
Loewner hulls and first hits of vertical lines, so dense self-touching
does not affect it.

Apply the identical argument to the centered future driver in (NS1).
Lemma~\ref{lem:ns-timezero-conditional} supplies the same crossing
constants after conditioning at $\tau$. This proves (NS4) for all
finite stopping times. Tail integration gives (NS5) for $0<a<c$
and the moment bounds. Kolmogorov continuity, using arbitrarily
large $p$, also gives pathwise H\"older exponents $\alpha<1/2$
on every compact capacity interval.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Relation to the existing literature and limits of the estimate.}
This is a direct version of the rectangle argument underlying
Kemppainen--Smirnov~\cite{ks}, Section 3.3, Propositions 3.8--3.9. Their
Theorem 1.5 starts with families of simple curves and obtains nonsimple
limits. One cannot apply that formulation merely by choosing
deterministic simple approximations of each sample: the approximating
\emph{laws} need not retain the conditional crossing bound. The direct
argument above instead assumes continuous-driver admissibility for
the continuum family and proves its quantitative estimate there.

These estimates control unsigned increments. They do not bound
$|\mathbb E[W_{t+h}-W_t\mid\mathcal F_t]|$ by $O(h)$,
establish semimartingality, or identify an affine conformal structure.
Uniform quad bounds are stable under a fixed quasiconformal change
with altered constants; Brownian-size bounds therefore also hold in
coordinates which need not be the correct affine coordinates for
stochastic driving rigidity.

\section{Unsigned regularity and the missing continuum signed budget}
\label{sec:continuum-regularity-gap}

% Independent continuum driving-regularity investigation, 28 October 2026.
% Fragment only: do not interpret the process example as a curve-family example.
\subsection{Unsigned Brownian-scale regularity does not supply a semimartingale}
\label{sec:unsigned-not-semimartingale}

The distinction between topological Loewner admissibility and stochastic
regularity is substantive even after strong quantitative estimates are
available. The following process example isolates the missing signed
cancellation. It is not asserted to obey source locality, domain Markov
for an undecorated slit domain, or a curve-level unforced-crossing estimate.

\begin{proposition}[A driver-level obstruction to an unsigned regularity argument]
\label{prop:unsigned-nonsemimartingale}
For every $\kappa>0$, $H\in(1/2,1)$, and $a\ne0$, there is a continuous
process $X$ on $[0,1]$ with all the following properties in its usual
natural filtration:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Its paths are locally $\alpha$-H\"older for every $\alpha<1/2$.
\item For every $c>0$ there is $C_c<\infty$ such that, for every stopping
 time $\tau$ and deterministic $h>0$ with $\tau+h\le1$ almost surely,
 \[
 \mathbb E\!\left[\exp\!\left(
   c\,\frac{\sup_{0\le u\le h}|X_{\tau+u}-X_\tau|}{\sqrt h}
                  \right)\,\middle|\,\mathcal F_\tau\right]\le C_c.
 \tag{UR1}
 \]
\item Its sums of squared increments along dyadic partitions have the
 same limit as Brownian motion of variance rate $\kappa$.
\item It is not a semimartingale.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Let $\varphi(x)=\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathbb Z)$ and define
\[
 g_H(t)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{-nH}\varphi(2^nt),
 \qquad X_t=\sqrt\kappa B_t+a g_H(t),
 \tag{UR2}
\]
where $B$ is standard Brownian motion. Uniform convergence defines a
continuous deterministic function. The bounds
$|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|\le\min\{|x-y|,1/2\}$ show
\[
 |g_H(t)-g_H(s)|\le C_H|t-s|^H.
 \tag{UR3}
\]
Indeed split the series where $2^n|t-s|$ first exceeds one, and sum the
finite geometric series of Lipschitz bounds and the remaining geometric
tail separately. Both sums are bounded by a constant times $|t-s|^H$.
The pathwise H\"older assertion follows.

The natural filtrations of $X$ and $B$ are identical, since the shift
$ag_H$ is deterministic. Brownian strong Markov and (UR3) give, for
$h\le1$, the bound in (UR1) by
\[
 e^{c|a|C_H}\,
 \mathbb E\exp\!\left(c\sqrt\kappa\sup_{0\le u\le1}|B_u|\right)<\infty.
\]
Reflection bounds for Brownian maxima make the last expectation finite
for every $c>0$.

Here is an explicit verification of the variation obstruction. Set
$q=2^{1-H}>1$, and use the mesh $t_j=j2^{-m}$, $0\le j\le2^m$.
All terms with $n\ge m$ vanish at the mesh points. On each mesh interval
all the earlier tent functions are linear, so
\[
 \Delta_j g_H
 =2^{-m}\sum_{n=0}^{m-1}q^n r_{n+1}(j),
 \tag{UR4}
\]
where the sign vectors $r_{n+1}$ are the dyadic Rademacher vectors.
Their orthogonality on this mesh gives
\[
 Q_m:=\sum_j(\Delta_jg_H)^2
   =2^{-m}\frac{q^{2m}-1}{q^2-1},\qquad
 \max_j|\Delta_jg_H|
   \le2^{-m}\frac{q^m-1}{q-1}.
 \tag{UR5}
\]
Consequently
\[
 \sum_j|\Delta_jg_H|
 \ge\frac{Q_m}{\max_j|\Delta_jg_H|}
 \ge\frac{q^m+1}{q+1}\longrightarrow\infty.
 \tag{UR6}
\]
Thus $g_H$ has infinite variation. On the other hand $Q_m\to0$ because
$H>1/2$. More generally (UR3) bounds the sums of squared increments on
any subinterval of the dyadic mesh by $C_H^2 2^{-m(2H-1)}$, so they tend
to zero uniformly over mesh endpoints. Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the mixed
Brownian/$g_H$ sums by the square root of the product of their squared
increment sums. The standard Brownian dyadic quadratic-variation limit
therefore proves the asserted Brownian quadratic variation for $X$.

For completeness, the failure of semimartingality can also be seen
without relying only on the deterministic infinite-variation criterion.
The bounded deterministic elementary predictable strategies
$L^m_t=\operatorname{sign}(a\Delta_j g_H)$ on
$(t_j,t_{j+1}]$ give the elementary sums
\[
 \sum_jL^m_{t_{j+1}}(X_{t_{j+1}}-X_{t_j})
   = |a|\sum_j|\Delta_jg_H|+Z_m,
 \tag{UR7}
\]
where $Z_m$ is centered Gaussian with variance at most $\kappa$.
They diverge to $+\infty$ in probability by (UR6). For a continuous
semimartingale, its elementary integrals with predictable integrands
bounded by one are bounded in probability on a fixed finite horizon:
localize the martingale bracket and finite-variation total variation,
then use the martingale $L^2$ inequality. This contradicts (UR7).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{What RSW regularity can legitimately supply.}
Kemppainen--Smirnov~\cite{ks}, Proposition 2.6, Theorem 1.5,
and Section 3.3, give conformally stable conditional unforced-crossing
criteria, continuous driving with every H\"older exponent below $1/2$,
and Brownian-scale exponential driving bounds in their stated setup.
Their Section 1.4 explicitly permits nonsimple curves already having
continuous Loewner drivers; it does not alone give that property for an
arbitrary law of uniformly approximable nonsimple curves. These are
unsigned estimates; they do not assert semimartingality.
The example above proves that even the listed stronger process-level
conclusions, including conditional estimates at stopping times and
Brownian quadratic variation, cannot replace the missing signed
cancellation argument. It is not a counterexample to the full curve axioms.

\subsection{A continuum signed budget on visited slit states}
\label{sec:continuum-signed-budget}

A useful first target is weaker than the uniform linear conditional-bias
bound (BI1). It asks for finite aggregate predictable variation, not for a
pointwise drift density. Its probabilistic content is classical
quasimartingale theory; the formulation below makes the exact continuum
restart observable and its stopping rule explicit.

Assume only the Borel continuous-law and restart-identity premises of
(KR1), not the semimartingale premises of Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-characteristics}.
Let $\{P_f\}$ be this family, with drivers
starting at zero and defined on $[0,\infty)$. Fix $L,T>0$, set
\[
 \sigma_L=\inf\{t\ge0:|W_t|\ge L\},\qquad
 X_t=W_{t\wedge\sigma_L},\quad 0\le t\le T,
 \tag{QV1}
\]
and use the usual natural filtration of the original $W$.
For $\ell<0<r$, define the bounded restarted mean
\[
 m_h(f;\ell,r)=\mathbb E_{P_f}
   W_{h\wedge\inf\{u:W_u\notin(\ell,r)\}}.
 \tag{QV2}
\]
This is a Borel function of its arguments: the stopped evaluation of a
continuous path at its first exit from an open interval is Borel and is
bounded between the two endpoints. No claim of continuity in $f$ is made.

\begin{proposition}[An exact signed restart budget]
\label{prop:continuum-quasimartingale}
Suppose (KR1) holds in the inherited centered capacity charts. For every
deterministic partition $\pi=(0=t_0<\cdots<t_n=T)$,
\[
 \mathbb E[X_{t_{j+1}}-X_{t_j}\mid\mathcal F_{t_j}]
 =\mathbf1_{\{t_j<\sigma_L\}}
 m_{t_{j+1}-t_j}(f_{t_j};-L-W_{t_j},L-W_{t_j}).
 \tag{QV3}
\]
Consequently the aggregate budget
\[
 \begin{split}
 \mathcal V_{L,T}(f)=\sup_\pi\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathbb E_{P_f}\bigl[
  \mathbf1_{\{t_j<\sigma_L\}}
  |m_{t_{j+1}-t_j}(f_{t_j};-L-W_{t_j},L-W_{t_j})|\bigr]
 \end{split}
 \tag{QV4}
\]
is exactly the conditional variation of $X$. It is finite if and only if
$X=M+A$, $A_0=0$, has a continuous canonical decomposition with $M$ a uniformly
integrable martingale on $[0,T]$ and
$\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}_{[0,T]}A<\infty$. In that case
\[
 \mathcal V_{L,T}(f)=\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}_{[0,T]}A.
 \tag{QV5}
\]
If it is finite for every $L,T$, every driver is a continuous
semimartingale locally. No absolute continuity of $A$, initial tangent,
or Brownian bracket follows just from this finiteness.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
On $\{t_j\ge\sigma_L\}$ the stopped increment vanishes. Otherwise freeze
the past at $t_j$. The conditional future driver is $P_{f_{t_j}}$ by
(KR1). The original stopping boundary $(-L,L)$ becomes exactly
$(-L-W_{t_j},L-W_{t_j})$ for that centered future driver. This proves
(QV3), hence the conditional-variation identity (QV4).

Since $X$ is bounded and continuous, the quasimartingale decomposition
theorem gives precisely the stated continuous canonical decomposition
when its conditional variation is finite; see Rao~\cite{rao}.
Conversely, given this decomposition, martingale increments have
conditional mean zero, so each partition sum is at most
$\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}A$.
For the reverse inequality, bounded predictable elementary processes on
deterministic time partitions are dense among bounded predictable
integrands for the finite measure
$H\mapsto\mathbb E\int_0^T H_s\,d|A|_s$.
Approximate the predictable sign of the signed measure $dA$ by such
processes, all bounded by one. For an elementary process with value
$H_j\in\mathcal F_{t_j}$ on $(t_j,t_{j+1}]$,
\[
 \mathbb E\int_0^T H_s\,dA_s
 =\sum_j\mathbb E[H_j(X_{t_{j+1}}-X_{t_j})]
 \le\sum_j\mathbb E\bigl|
     \mathbb E[X_{t_{j+1}}-X_{t_j}\mid\mathcal F_{t_j}]\bigr|.
\]
The approximation gives (QV5). The local conclusion follows by taking
$L\uparrow\infty$ and $T\uparrow\infty$, using continuity and uniqueness
of continuous canonical decompositions.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Why this is a weaker target, and why it is still missing.}
The bound (BI1) gives $\mathcal V_{L,T}\le KT$ and thus suffices. Finiteness
of (QV4) alone also permits singular finite-variation drift. For example,
Brownian motion plus a deterministic Cantor function has finite conditional
variation on compact intervals but its canonical drift is not absolutely
continuous. This example illustrates the distinction between the two
probabilistic conditions, not a proposed exploration family.
An unsigned estimate of Brownian order gives only a bound of order
$\sum_j\sqrt{t_{j+1}-t_j}$, which diverges on finer equal partitions.
The budget must be proved from interactions of locality and DMP, in a
suitable common affine structure; none of those additional cancellation
estimates has been established here. One cannot replace the offsets in
(QV3) by the fresh symmetric interval $(-L,L)$: the localization boundary
is inherited from the past.

\subsection{The shrinking-slit anomaly fits inside Brownian-size bounds}
\label{sec:slit-pulse-obstruction}

\begin{proposition}[Uniform unsigned bounds do not control initial bias rates]
\label{prop:slit-pulse}
Let $b_n\ne0$ with $|b_n|\to\infty$, and let $\kappa>0$. Put
\[
 a_n(t)=b_n(t\wedge b_n^{-2}),\qquad
 X^n_t=\sqrt\kappa B_t+a_n(t),\quad t\ge0.
 \tag{SP1}
\]
Then $\sup_t|X^n_t-\sqrt\kappa B_t|\le|b_n|^{-1}\to0$ under the displayed
coupling. Uniformly in $n$, the processes satisfy the conditional
Brownian-scale exponential bound (UR1), at every bounded stopping time
and every deterministic $h>0$. Every $X^n$ is a semimartingale with
bracket $\kappa t$, but its initial drift is $b_n$. The optimal constant
in its unstopped linear conditional-bias bound is $K_n=|b_n|$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The deterministic shifts obey
\[
 |a_n(t)-a_n(s)|
 \le\min\{|b_n||t-s|,|b_n|^{-1}\}\le\sqrt{|t-s|}.
 \tag{SP2}
\]
Brownian strong Markov therefore gives (UR1), uniformly in $n$, by adding
one inside the normalized supremum. The uniform-convergence and bracket
claims are immediate. For $0<h<b_n^{-2}$ the initial mean increment is
$b_nh$, proving the optimal lower bound on $K_n$; the Lipschitz bound
$|b_n|$ proves the upper bound.
\end{proof}

For the mirrored slit derivatives in (DR9), a variance anomaly produces
\[
 b_\epsilon^\pm=(\kappa/2-3)\frac{e_\epsilon^\pm}{d_\epsilon^2},
 \qquad
 \frac{e_\epsilon^\pm}{d_\epsilon^2}
 =\mp\frac4{\epsilon^3\sqrt{\epsilon^2+4}}.
 \tag{SP3}
\]
For $\kappa\ne6$ these are of order $\epsilon^{-3}$. Substituting either
sequence into (SP1) gives pulses of duration $\asymp\epsilon^6$ and
height $\asymp\epsilon^3$. Thus even uniform convergence to Brownian
motion, exact Brownian bracket, and uniform conditional Brownian-size
bounds allow precisely the inverse-cubic initial-bias scale which the
slit test needs to exclude. Choosing $b_\epsilon=b/d_\epsilon$ similarly
realizes the inverse-linear drift scale. The sharp corner at the end
of a pulse can be smoothed without changing these orders.

These processes are not claimed to be the locality-related drivers of
one exploration family. The point is narrower and rigorous: neither
uniform unsigned driving estimates nor weak continuity of the driver
laws implies the rate $\epsilon K_\epsilon\to0$ required in (BI5).
A successful shrinking-slit proof must use additional structure of the
locality comparison, not only the size and continuity conclusions of RSW.


% Proposed result for independent checking; no tracked source edited.
\section{What the continuum crossing estimates give once the signed budget is finite}
\label{sec:bracket-from-crossing-moments}

The finite aggregate signed budget supplies a continuous
semimartingale, but by itself it supplies no regularity of its canonical
characteristics. The unsigned continuum estimate nevertheless supplies
one additional conclusion at that point: the bracket is absolutely
continuous with a bounded density. This does not require a tangent of
the drift, or any regularity of its total variation.

\begin{proposition}[Brownian-order conditional moments dominate the bracket]
\label{prop:moment-bracket-domination}
Let $X$ be a continuous semimartingale in a usual filtration, with
canonical decomposition $X=X_0+M+A$, where $M$ is a continuous local
martingale starting at zero and $A$ is continuous predictable finite
variation. Suppose that for every $0\le s<t\le T$,
\[
 \mathbb E\bigl[(X_t-X_s)^2\mid\mathcal F_s\bigr]
       \le K(t-s)\quad\hbox{almost surely}
 \tag{BR1}
\]
with one deterministic finite $K$. Then, pathwise,
\[
 d\langle M\rangle_t\le K\,dt.
 \tag{BR2}
\]
Consequently $M$ is square integrable on $[0,T]$ and its bracket has
a predictable density $v$ with $0\le v\le K$ almost everywhere.
No bound on the variation or initial tangent of $A$ is asserted.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Write $C=\langle M\rangle$. For fixed deterministic $s<t$, the
quadratic increment sums along refining deterministic partitions of
$[s,t]$ converge in probability to $C_t-C_s$. For any
$E\in\mathcal F_s$, the tower property and (BR1) give
\[
 \mathbb E\left[\mathbf1_E\sum_i
       (X_{t_{i+1}}-X_{t_i})^2\right]
       \le K(t-s)\mathbb P(E).
\]
Choose an almost-surely converging subsequence of the nonnegative
sums and apply Fatou. Thus
\[
 \mathbb E[\mathbf1_E(C_t-C_s)]
       \le K(t-s)\mathbb P(E).
 \tag{BR3}
\]
In particular $\mathbb E C_T\le KT$, and $C_t-Kt$ is a
continuous supermartingale of class D: all of its stopped absolute
values are dominated by $C_T+KT$.

A continuous predictable finite-variation supermartingale of class D
is nonincreasing: its existing special-semimartingale decomposition has
zero martingale part, and uniqueness identifies its drift with the
negative increasing Doob--Meyer compensator. Apply this fact to
$C_t-Kt$ to obtain (BR2). Finite expected bracket makes $M$ square
integrable, and the predictable Radon--Nikodym density of $dC$ gives
the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Application to the continuum classification problem.}
The conditional exponential estimate already proved in the nonsimple
curve class implies (BR1) with a uniform numerical constant depending
only on the crossing constants. If the aggregate signed restart budget
is finite for a bounded stopped driver, then the quasimartingale
criterion supplies its canonical decomposition. Such a stopped driver
retains (BR1): on $\{\sigma_L>s\}$ its increment is bounded by the
original driver supremum over $[s,t]$, and otherwise it vanishes. The proposition then
gives bounded absolutely continuous bracket automatically. Thus the
first signed-budget target, if established, leaves drift regularity
as the characteristic bottleneck; it does not leave both bracket
regularity and drift regularity independent.

\begin{corollary}[The remaining singular drift is transported without an anomaly]
\label{cor:singular-drift-transport}
Assume the proposition for all relevant chart drivers. Before contact
with a locality deletion, let $h_t$ be the evolving deletion map and
$u'(t)=h_t'(W_t)^2>0$. Write
$dA=b_t\,dt+dA^\perp_t$ for the decomposition relative to capacity
Lebesgue measure. Then the restarted deleted-chart characteristics
satisfy
\[
 d\widehat A_{u(t)}
   =h_t'(W_t)\,dA_t
       +\tfrac12h_t''(W_t)(v_t-6)\,dt,
 \qquad
 d\widehat A^\perp_{u(t)}
   =h_t'(W_t)\,dA^\perp_t.
 \tag{BR4}
\]
In particular the deletion cannot create or cancel a singular drift
component by the variance-six anomaly. The singular part transforms
as a one-form, with its capacity clock changed as stated.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The deletion It\^o identity is
\[
 d\widehat W_{u(t)}
 =h_t'(W_t)\,dM_t+h_t'(W_t)\,dA_t
     +\tfrac12h_t''(W_t)\,dC_t-3h_t''(W_t)\,dt.
\]
Insert $dC=v\,dt$ and use uniqueness of canonical decomposition.
The strictly positive continuous capacity-clock derivative makes
$u$ locally bi-Lipschitz before deletion contact, so it preserves
the distinction between absolutely continuous and singular measures.
All terms except the indicated pushforward of $dA^\perp$ are
absolutely continuous. This proves (BR4).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{A failed uniform-variation shortcut.}
An unscaled bound on total drift variation in mirrored shrinking
deletion charts does not by itself give the local first-order rates
needed by the existing rigidity theorem. The anomaly can be supported
on capacity windows much shorter than the slit scale. The previously
proved pulse example shows why bounded variation budgets alone cannot
be replaced silently by drift-density bounds. Conversely, imposing a
uniform $o(\sqrt t)$ initial variation modulus over \emph{all}
positive scalings of every chart would be far too strong: if
$A^{(\lambda)}_{\lambda^2t}=\lambda A_t$, then
\[
 \lambda\,\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}(A;[0,T])
 \le\omega(\lambda^2T)\lambda\sqrt T,
       \qquad\omega(r)\longrightarrow0,
\]
and $\lambda\downarrow0$ already forces $A=0$, without locality.
Any useful added variation premise must therefore specify its chart
normalization and its domain dependence rather than hide an
all-scalings martingale assumption in a uniform modulus.

\paragraph{Remaining research question.}
Can source locality, physical translation covariance, and the precise
crossing hypothesis control the finite signed budget, or rule out the
one-form-valued singular drift in (BR4), in one fixed unknown affine
structure? The present proposition does not answer either question.
It narrows the required characteristic input once semimartingality
has been obtained.

% Continuum locality / signed-budget investigation, 28 October 2026.
% Candidate fragment for independent checking; no tracked files edited.
% New proposed locality argument; requires independent check.
\paragraph{Why an interior-hitting shortcut would be invalid.}
The boundary-unforced crossing estimate alone does not imply a positive
lower driving tail: it includes deterministic SLE$_0$ among its examples.
In the Kemppainen--Smirnov definition the unforced annulus part is empty
when the inner circle misses the current domain boundary; see
\cite[Definition 2.3 and Section 1.4]{ks}. Thus an argument treating first
entry into an interior ball as an already controlled unforced crossing
would add an unproved premise. The next ray-deletion comparison repairs
the particular driving-confinement argument by using corrected locality;
it does not assert general interior small-ball nonconcentration.

\subsection{Source locality and a ray deletion give a lower driving tail}
\label{sec:locality-ray-lower-tail}

The upper unforced-quad estimate alone does not control first entry
into an interior ball. The following argument uses source locality
essentially, turning the relevant interior point into a boundary slit
tip in a comparison domain. It proves a lower \emph{driving} tail,
not a general interior small-ball nonconcentration estimate.

\paragraph{Admissible comparison states.}
For a physical inverse chart $f:\mathbb H\to D$ and $r>0$, require
that the domain
\[
 D'=f(\mathbb H\setminus\{iy:y\ge r\})
 \tag{RL1}
\]
belongs to the family, with source $f(0)$ and either of the two
appropriate target prime ends over the original target infinity.
It is enough to use the right-hand target end. A canonical inverse
chart for this comparison domain is $f\circ F_r$, where
\[
 F_r(w)=\frac r2\left(\sqrt{w+1}-\frac1{\sqrt{w+1}}\right),
 \tag{RL1a}
\]
with the principal square root. Indeed the square root maps the half-plane
onto a quadrant, and $s\mapsto(r/2)(s-1/s)$ maps that quadrant onto
the ray-deleted half-plane, taking $1$ to $0$. This also provides a
jointly Borel comparison state and target choice. Choose boundary colors
so they agree with the original colors on the two real sides near zero. Source locality is assumed to compare
the ordered prefixes up to first encounter with the removed ray.
For regular distinct source and target points this is a usual
source-neighborhood deletion. At rough states, this is an explicit
prime-end/source-deletion admissibility requirement, not a consequence
of Euclidean neighborhood locality at coincident impressions.

\begin{lemma}[A lower driving tail from locality and boundary quads]
\label{lem:ray-locality-driver-lower}
Assume continuous Loewner generation with capacity $2t$, corrected
source locality for (RL1), and the uniform boundary-robust unforced
quad bound with constants $M>0$, $q<1$ on all these states.
Put
\[
 \delta=\frac14e^{-2\pi M}.
 \tag{RL2}
\]
Then, for every $h>0$,
\[
 \mathbb P\left\{\sup_{0\le s\le h}|W_s|>
                       \delta\sqrt h\right\}\ge1-q.
 \tag{RL3}
\]
Strong DMP gives the same conditional bound in the inherited centered
chart after any finite stopping time.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
In the original half-plane chart set $z=i\sqrt h$ and delete the
upward ray $A=\{iy:y\ge\sqrt h\}$. The comparison domain is
$U=\mathbb H\setminus A$. It is simply connected. Its two ends at
infinity are distinguished prime ends; choosing either one as target
allows the boundary colors on the real line to match the original
source colors. Corrected source locality couples the original trace
and the comparison trace through their first hit of $A$.

Let $E$ be the event $\sup_{s\le h}|W_s|\le\delta\sqrt h$.
On $E$, the original trace and hull up to time $h$ lie in the vertical
strip $|\operatorname{Re}w|\le\delta\sqrt h$ by the Loewner ODE.
If its trace height were at most $\sqrt h$, its hull would lie in a
disk of radius $\sqrt{(1+\delta^2)h}$. The area theorem would give
$2h\le(1+\delta^2)h$, impossible because $\delta<1$.
Consequently the trace reaches height $\sqrt h$ by time $h$ and
there enters $B(z,2\delta\sqrt h)$.

Entry into the open ball occurs strictly before the first encounter
with $A$. If the first ray encounter before $h$ is outside this ball,
its height is greater than $\sqrt h$, so an earlier height-$\sqrt h$
point was already in the ball. If the first encounter itself lies in
the ball, continuity puts an earlier point in that open ball. If there
is no ray encounter before $h$, the asserted visit already precedes it.
Thus the coupled comparison trace visits the ball on $E$, using only
the common prefix; $E$ itself need not be a common-prefix event.

In $U$, consider the annulus centered at $z$ of inner radius
$2\delta\sqrt h$ and outer radius $\sqrt h/2$, slit along the
upward ray. It is a boundary quad of opening angle $2\pi$, with
both side arcs on the two prime-end sides of $A$. Its modulus is
\[
 \frac1{2\pi}\log\frac{\sqrt h/2}{2\delta\sqrt h}
       =M.
\]
It is unforced: source zero and either target end at infinity can
be connected outside its outer circle, on the chosen target side.
The comparison trace starts outside its outer circle, so visiting
the inner ball requires a crossing. The boundary-robust convention
counts side contacts and a visit ending at the slit tip as well.
The quad bound gives comparison visit probability at most $q$.
Hence $\mathbb P(E)\le q$, proving (RL3).

The same comparison can be made in every inherited restart chart.
Genuine strong DMP therefore supplies the conditional version with
the same constants. No conformal covariance of the physical family
was used.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{What this does and does not establish.}
Together with the upper bracket theorem and the stochastic lower-tail
criterion, this gives uniformly elliptic bracket for a
semimartingale driver from the current crossing bound and the stated
ray-deletion locality. It does not identify the density as six,
remove singular drift, or prove semimartingality. In particular it
does not infer a general interior hitting estimate from the
boundary-quad condition. Deterministic vertical SLE$_0$ fails the
source-locality comparison used here, so it does not contradict this
argument.

\subsection{The driving variation index is two before semimartingality}
\label{sec:driver-variation-index}

The lower driving tail has a consequence which does not assume a
finite signed budget, a bracket, or a semimartingale decomposition.
For a continuous real path $x$ on $[a,b]$, write
\[
 V_r(x;[a,b])=\sup_\pi\sum_j|x(t_{j+1})-x(t_j)|^r,
       \qquad r>0,
 \tag{VI1}
\]
where the supremum is over finite partitions of that interval.

\begin{proposition}[Uniform diffusive lower tails force variation index two]
\label{prop:driver-variation-index}
Let $X$ be continuous and adapted, and suppose there are fixed
$\delta,p>0$ such that for all deterministic $t\ge0$ and $h>0$,
\[
 \mathbb P\{\sup_{0\le u\le h}|X_{t+u}-X_t|
                 >\delta\sqrt h\mid\mathcal F_t\}\ge p.
 \tag{VI2}
\]
Then, on one probability-one event, $V_r(X;[a,b])=\infty$ for
every nontrivial interval and every $0<r<2$. If its paths are also
locally H\"older for every exponent below $1/2$, then on that same
event $V_r(X;[a,b])<\infty$ on every compact interval for every
$r>2$. Thus the variation index is exactly two on every nontrivial
interval. No assertion about $V_2$ or a quadratic-variation limit
is made.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Fix first a deterministic compact interval $[a,b]$, put
$n=2^m$, $h=(b-a)/n$, $t_j=a+jh$, and define
\[
 I_j=\mathbf1_{\{\sup_{0\le u\le h}
                  |X_{t_j+u}-X_{t_j}|>\delta\sqrt h\}}.
\]
These indicators are measurable at $t_{j+1}$, and
$\mathbb E[I_j\mid\mathcal F_{t_j}]\ge p$.
The martingale differences
$D_j=I_j-\mathbb E[I_j\mid\mathcal F_{t_j}]$ are orthogonal in
$L^2$ and satisfy $\mathbb E(\sum_jD_j)^2\le n$. Therefore
\[
 \mathbb P\{\textstyle\sum_jI_j<pn/2\}
     \le\frac4{p^2n}.
 \tag{VI3}
\]
This is summable over dyadic $n$. Borel--Cantelli gives
$\sum_jI_j\ge pn/2$ for all sufficiently large $m$, almost surely.
For every successful block, choose a time where its displacement
from the left endpoint exceeds $\delta\sqrt h$. The ordered union
of these times and the block endpoints is a finite partition, so
simultaneously for every $r>0$,
\[
 V_r(X;[a,b])\ge\frac{pn}{2}\delta^r h^{r/2}
       =\frac{p\delta^r}{2}(b-a)^{r/2}n^{1-r/2}.
 \tag{VI4}
\]
No adapted selection is needed: this is a pathwise lower bound for a
supremum over partitions. It diverges for every $r<2$ on the same
event. Intersect over rational intervals, and place a rational
subinterval inside any other nontrivial interval.

If $r>2$, choose $1/r<\alpha<1/2$. A H\"older-$\alpha$ constant
$K$ on a compact interval bounds every partition sum by
$K^r\sum_j|t_{j+1}-t_j|^{\alpha r}\le K^r(b-a)^{\alpha r}$.
The simultaneous upper assertion follows from the countable collection
of rational H\"older exponents.
\end{proof}

By the ray-locality lemma and the already proved upper driving bounds,
the continuum family has this variation index under the stated
ray-deletion admissibility. In particular its drivers cannot have
finite variation on any nontrivial interval. The lower proof controls
block oscillations, not terminal increments of the blocks; replacing
it by a claim of positive quadratic variation along deterministic
partitions would be unjustified. Nor does variation index two imply
semimartingality: the earlier Brownian-plus-Takagi example already
shows the latter conclusion needs signed information.

\subsection{A bounded bracket becomes uniformly elliptic}
\label{sec:lower-bracket-from-locality}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional lower tails make a bounded bracket elliptic]
\label{prop:bracket-lower-tail}
Let $X=X_0+M+A$ be a continuous semimartingale with
$d\langle M\rangle=v_tdt$, $0\le v_t\le K$, and $A$ continuous finite
variation. Suppose the conditional lower-tail bound (RL3), with lower probability
$p>0$, holds with $X$ in place of $W$, at least at
every deterministic $\tau=t$ and for all $h>0$ in a finite horizon.
Then
\[
 v_t\ge \frac{p\delta^2}{32}
       \quad dt\otimes d\mathbb P\hbox{-almost everywhere}.
 \tag{NC3}
\]
No density or initial tangent of $A$ is assumed.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Choose a bounded predictable version of $v$. For Lebesgue-almost every
deterministic $t$, Fubini and Lebesgue differentiation give, almost surely,
\[
 \frac1h\int_t^{t+h}v_sds\longrightarrow v_t,
 \qquad
 \frac{\operatorname{Var}(A;[t,t+h])}{\sqrt h}\longrightarrow0.
 \tag{NC4}
\]
The second assertion follows because the finite Stieltjes variation
measure has a finite one-sided Lebesgue derivative almost everywhere.
Fix such a $t$, set $\eta=p\delta^2/32$, and let
$E=\{v_t<\eta\}\in\mathcal F_t$. The lower tail assumption implies
\[
 p\mathbb P(E)\le
 \mathbb P\{E,\sup_{u\le h}|X_{t+u}-X_t|>\delta\sqrt h\}.
\]
The corresponding drift supremum is at most
$\operatorname{Var}(A;[t,t+h])$, so its probability of exceeding
$\delta\sqrt h/2$ tends to zero by (NC4). Since $v\le K$, $M$ is
square integrable on finite horizons and Doob's inequality gives
\[
 \mathbb P\{E,\sup_{u\le h}|M_{t+u}-M_t|>\delta\sqrt h/2\}
 \le\frac{16}{\delta^2h}
       \mathbb E[\mathbf1_E(C_{t+h}-C_t)].
\]
Boundedness of $v$ and (NC4) permit dominated convergence of the last
normalized expectation to $\mathbb E[\mathbf1_Ev_t]$.
Thus $p\mathbb P(E)\le16\eta\mathbb P(E)/\delta^2
=(p/2)\mathbb P(E)$, forcing $\mathbb P(E)=0$. Apply Fubini again.
\end{proof}

Combining this proposition with the upper bracket theorem and the
ray-locality lemma gives $c\,dt\le dC\le K\,dt$, with
$c=(1-q)\delta^2/32>0$, once the finite signed budget supplies
semimartingality. The density is not yet identified as six. The lower
conclusion uses corrected ray-deletion locality, not the boundary-quad
estimate alone.

\subsection{Bounded Brownian bracket does not remove a singular drift}
\label{sec:reflected-driver-obstruction}

The reflected Brownian process $X_t=|B_t|$ is a sharp process-level
warning at the next stage. In its usual natural filtration it has
canonical decomposition $X=N+\Lambda$, where $N$ is Brownian motion
and $\Lambda$ is the continuous Skorokhod regulator, increasing only
on $\{X=0\}$. That set has zero Lebesgue measure almost surely, so
$d\Lambda$ is singular and nonzero. Tanaka's formula gives the
decomposition in the Brownian filtration; the regulator is recovered
from the occupation local time of $X$, so both summands are adapted
to its smaller natural filtration, where the martingale identity
continues to hold by conditioning.

For every stopping time $\tau$ of $X$,
\[
 \sup_{u\le h}\bigl||B_{\tau+u}|-|B_\tau|\bigr|
 \le\sup_{u\le h}|B_{\tau+u}-B_\tau|.
 \tag{RB1}
\]
Brownian strong Markov and conditioning therefore give uniform
conditional Gaussian-size increment bounds for $X$, even though its
finite signed budget is carried by a singular regulator. In particular
its bracket density is exactly one. This is not a curve-family
counterexample: neither source locality nor the domain-indexed
exploration axioms are asserted for this example. It shows why bounded
bracket and unsigned driving estimates do not finish the drift argument.

\section{Analytic locality and signed characteristic measures}
\label{sec:analytic-locality-budget}

\paragraph{Scope and matched localization.}
This section uses source locality only to identify two initial ordered
trace laws and their natural filtrations. On their common trace, let
$h_t$ be the evolving analytic deletion map, recentered at the source,
and write
\[
 p_t=h_t'(W_t)>0,\quad q_t=h_t''(W_t),\quad
 u(t)=\int_0^t p_s^2\,ds,\quad
 Y_t=\widehat W_{u(t)}=h_t(W_t)-h_0(0).
 \tag{LB1}
\]
The exact recentering convention has no effect on the derivatives or
characteristics. The two capacity clocks and their inverses are
stopping-time clocks in the corresponding natural trace filtrations,
by geometric reconstruction of a stopped trace. Stop on a strictly
positive interval where the deletion has not collided with the trace,
its field and inverse are absolutely continuous in time as
$C^2$-valued fields on compact analytic source neighborhoods, with
bounded spatial derivatives, and
\[
 |W|\le L,\quad |Y|\le\widehat L,\quad
 0<m\le p\le P,\quad |q|\le Q,\quad \theta\le T.
 \tag{LB2}
\]
All identities below concern the processes stopped at this same physical
trace time $\theta$, not two independently selected exit rules.
The inherited stop $u(\theta)$ is a stopping time in deleted capacity,
and $u(\theta)\le P^2T$.
The deletion PDE gives the stated time regularity before collision.
In particular, the time dependence of the field is pathwise finite variation;
no stochastic regularity of $W$ is assumed in writing (LB1).

\begin{proposition}[Local semimartingality and finite signed budgets are analytic-locality invariants]
\label{prop:locality-budget-transfer}
Under this setup, the original driver is locally a continuous
semimartingale if and only if the deleted driver is, on the matched
initial intervals. Suppose they have canonical decompositions
$W=M+A$ and $\widehat W=\widehat M+\widehat A$, with brackets
$C$ and $\widehat C$. Set $D_t=\widehat A_{u(t)}$. Then, before the
matched stop,
\[
 dD=p\,dA+\frac q2(dC-6dt),\qquad
 d\widehat C_{u(t)}=p^2\,dC.
 \tag{LB3}
\]
For the bounded stopped processes let $V=\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}A$
and $\widehat V=\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}D$, equivalently their
conditional-variation budgets when finite. Then
\[
 \widehat V\le(P+LQ)V+\tfrac12QL^2+3QT,
 \tag{LB4}
\]
and
\[
 V\le\left(m^{-1}+Q\widehat Lm^{-3}\right)\widehat V
       +\tfrac12Q\widehat L^2m^{-3}+3QT/m.
 \tag{LB5}
\]
In particular finite expected canonical variation, hence finite aggregate
signed budget, transfers both ways after these matched bounded
localizations. Neither estimate supplies a first-order time rate.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Assume first $W$ is a semimartingale. The finite-variation-field
It\^o--Wentzell formula applied to $h_t(W_t)$ gives
\[
 dY_t=p_t\,dM_t+p_t\,dA_t+\tfrac12q_t\,dC_t-3q_tdt.
\]
Here the final term is the Loewner deletion field identity
$(\partial_t h_t)(W_t)=-3h_t''(W_t)$, which requires no stochastic
calculus to derive. The geometric stopping-time clocks transfer the
semimartingale and its natural filtration to deleted capacity. Conversely,
if the deleted driver is a semimartingale, its pullback $Y$ is one in
the common trace filtration. Apply the same finite-variation-field
formula to $h_t^{-1}(Y_t+h_0(0))$. The inverse field is adapted,
spatially analytic, and of finite variation in time on this interval;
its derivatives are bounded under localization. Thus $W$ is a
semimartingale. Uniqueness of canonical continuous decompositions now
gives (LB3), without assuming any drift density or initial tangent.

We use a useful elementary budget/bracket estimate. If a continuous
semimartingale $Z=N+F$ starts at zero, is bounded by $K$, and has
$\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}F<\infty$, then
\[
 \mathbb E\langle N\rangle_T
   \le K^2+2K\mathbb E\operatorname{Var}F.
